Tool reply2026-09-12T23:15:46.338Z
1	# Faultline — architecture
2	
3	> Companion to `PLAN.md` (the living checklist). This document describes *how the system is built and
4	> why*; the plan tracks *what is left to do*. When a decision here changes, update §9 and the plan
5	> together. Status of facts below: verified against `modal==1.5.5`, node 21.6, pnpm 8.6 and the public
6	> docs on 2026-09-12. Secret name in Modal env `local`: `anthropic-secret`.
7	
8	## 1. What it is
9	
10	Faultline is a mini agent harness with a browser view. A Claude agent runs real shell commands (read
11	files, edit code, run tests) inside an isolated Modal Sandbox while the environment deliberately injects
12	failures at the tool boundary: a missing file, a denied write, a write that lands but whose acknowledgement
13	times out. The browser shows the run as a chat transcript (ChatGPT / claude.ai shape): the task prompt,
14	every model turn, every tool call with its output and any fault that hit it, the workspace diff, and a
15	graded score. Runs are grouped into conversations that belong to an anonymous browser identity and are
16	persisted in SQLite on a Modal Volume, so a reviewer can close the tab, come back, and re-read the
17	transcript.
18	
19	Three deployables, one repo:
20	
21	| Deployable | Modal app | Purpose |
22	|---|---|---|
23	| `apps/web` | `faultline-web` | static Vite + React SPA (shadcn/ui + Beautiful UI) served by a Modal Server |
24	| `services/agent-harness` | `faultline-harness` | HTTP API, the model loop (`run_episode`), and the persistence actor (`Store`) |
25	| `services/sandbox-env` | `faultline-sandbox-env` | the gym: episodes, fault plan, MCP tools, grader; drives Modal Sandboxes |
26	
27	Shared code: `packages/common/faultline_common` (`schemas.py` = wire contracts, `log.py` = unified
28	JSON-lines logging), mounted into every image.
29	
30	## 2. Topology and trust boundaries
31	
32	```
33	 browser  (apps/web on Modal Server)  ── anonymous id in localStorage + cookie mirror
34	    │  HTTPS JSON + SSE (reconnecting, Last-Event-ID); every request: X-Faultline-User: u_<uuid>
35	    ▼
36	 faultline-harness
37	    ├─ api          FastAPI @modal.asgi_app. No provider secret. Validates the user id, owns the HTTP
38	    │               contract (§5), spawns run_episode, and reads/writes state ONLY through Store.
39	    ├─ Store        @app.cls(max_containers=1, min_containers=1, volumes={"/data": faultline-db}).
40	    │               The single SQLite writer (§4). No secret, no fault plan, no network beyond Modal.
41	    └─ run_episode  @app.function(secrets=[anthropic-secret]). The ONLY code that sees ANTHROPIC_API_KEY.
42	                    Model loop over MCP tools; appends events to Store; evaluates; always deletes the episode.
43	    │  HTTPS: gym REST (reset / observe / evaluate / delete) + MCP streamable HTTP at /mcp (step)
44	    ▼
45	 faultline-sandbox-env
46	    └─ api          FastAPI + FastMCP. Stateless containers; episode state {sandbox_id, fault_plan,
47	                    ledger, baseline} in modal.Dict "faultline-episodes". Every tool call: load episode →
48	                    consult fault plan → (maybe) exec in the Sandbox → append to the ledger.
49	    │  modal.Sandbox.from_id(sid).exec(...)   block_network=True, no secrets, one per episode
50	    ▼
51	 Modal Sandbox      fixture repo at /workspace, python3 + pytest. Sees nothing else.
52	```
53	
54	What each process can see:
55	
56	| Process | Provider key | Fault plan / grader | Workspace files | Persistent DB | Network |
57	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
58	| browser | ✗ | ✗ (events about faults, after the fact) | via observe/events | via api only | harness only |
59	| harness `api` | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | via Store RPC | Store + spawn |
60	| harness `Store` | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ (only what events carry) | ✓ (sole writer) | Volume only |
61	| harness `run_episode` | ✓ | ✗ | via MCP only | via Store RPC | Anthropic + sandbox-env |
62	| sandbox-env `api` | ✗ | ✓ | via Sandbox API | ✗ | Modal Sandbox only |
63	| Modal Sandbox (agent's shell) | ✗ | ✗ | ✓ | ✗ | none |
64	
65	Why three apps: the harness must be replaceable (different model / prompt) without touching the
66	environment; the environment must be gradeable without trusting the agent; the UI is static. The secret
67	boundary is enforced by Modal's per-function secrets: `api` asserts at startup that `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`
68	is *absent* from its environment and reports `has_provider_key: false` on `/health`.
69	
70	## 3. Life of a run
71	
72	1. **Identity.** On first load the SPA mints `u_<uuid4>`, stores it in `localStorage["faultline.user_id"]`
73	   and mirrors it to the `faultline_uid` cookie. Every API call carries `X-Faultline-User`. `GET /me`
74	   upserts the `users` row.
75	2. **Conversation.** The user picks a scenario in the composer. `POST /conversations {scenario_id}` creates
76	   a `conversations` row titled after the scenario. A conversation is a thread of runs; the user's
77	   "message" is the task prompt (default = scenario prompt, editable before Run).
78	3. **Run.** `POST /conversations/{id}/runs {model?, seed?, max_steps?, task_prompt?}` → Store creates a
79	   `runs` row (`queued`) → `api` calls `run_episode.spawn(run_id, …)` and returns `{run_id}` immediately
80	   (Modal web requests are capped at 150 s, so nothing long-running happens inside a request).
81	4. **Episode.** `run_episode` calls `POST /episodes` on sandbox-env (reset: create Sandbox, upload
82	   fixture, apply sticky faults, snapshot baseline), emits `run.started` and `episode.reset`, then loops:
83	   `messages.create` → for each `tool_use` call the MCP tool with `X-Faultline-Episode` → emit
84	   `tool.call` / `tool.result` (+ `fault.fired` from the ledger delta, `workspace.diff` from observe after
85	   mutating calls) → repeat until `submit`, `end_turn`, `max_steps` or an unrecoverable API error.
86	   Every `messages.create` also emits `llm.call` (usage, stop reason, request id, attempt).
87	5. **Persistence.** Events are appended to Store in per-step batches
88	   (`Store().append_events.remote(run_id, events)`), which inserts `events` rows and, in the same
89	   transaction, projects them into `messages` / `blocks` / `llm_calls` (§4.5). The loop keeps the events in
90	   memory too and re-sends the full list at `run.finished` (idempotent), so a Store restart self-heals.
91	6. **Streaming.** The browser tails `GET /runs/{id}/events` (SSE). `api` polls `Store.events_after(run_id,
92	   after_seq)` every 400 ms, closes the stream at ~110 s, and the browser reconnects with
93	   `Last-Event-ID` (= `events.seq`). A page refresh restores from `GET /runs/{id}` (whole record) or
94	   `GET /conversations/{id}` (persisted transcript) and resumes the tail.
95	7. **Grading.** `POST /episodes/{id}/evaluate` uploads hidden tests, runs pytest, removes them, and scores
96	   recovery checks from the ledger (`score = 60·tests_pass + 40·weighted checks`). `episode.evaluated`
97	   and `run.finished` update `runs.score / evaluation_json / status / tokens`; `DELETE /episodes/{id}`
98	   runs in `finally`.
99	8. **Checkpoint.** Store snapshots the DB to the Volume after `run.finished` (§4.3).
100	
101	## 4. Persistence layer: SQLite on a Modal Volume
102	
103	### 4.1 Requirements
104	
105	Persist, per anonymous browser user: conversations, runs, the full event log of each run, and the LLM
106	turns including tool calls and their results, in a shape that can (a) render a chat transcript without
107	replaying events, and (b) rebuild the exact `messages=[…]` array the model saw at each step. Survive page
108	reloads, browser restarts, and redeploys. No external database service.
109	
110	### 4.2 Modal facts that constrain the design
111	
112	- Volumes are "write-once, read-many". Writes are visible to other containers only after a commit
113	  (Modal background-commits every few seconds and on container shutdown; `.commit()` forces one).
114	  Other containers must `.reload()` to see committed changes, and reload fails while files are open.
115	- "Last write wins in case of concurrent modification of the same file" — there is no distributed file
116	  locking. Concurrent writers to one SQLite file would corrupt it.
117	- The mount is a FUSE filesystem. SQLite's WAL mode relies on a shared-memory-mapped `-shm` file and
118	  POSIX advisory locks; their behaviour on the mount is undocumented. A background commit is not a
119	  point-in-time snapshot across files, so a live DB + its journal could be captured inconsistently.
120	- Modal's own SQLite example (the Datasette cron) builds the database on local disk, copies it onto the
121	  Volume, and commits. That is the pattern to copy.
122	- Volume v2 must be requested explicitly (`version=2`); the 1.5.5 CLI still marks it experimental. v1 is
123	  fine for one file.
124	
125	### 4.3 Design: one writer, hot copy on local disk, consistent snapshots on the Volume
126	
127	```
128	 api ──.remote()──▶ ┌───────────────── Store (1 container) ─────────────────┐
129	 run_episode ──────▶│ /tmp/faultline.sqlite3   WAL, synchronous=NORMAL, FK on │
130	                    │   ▲ restore on @modal.enter        checkpoint ▼         │
131	                    │ /data/faultline.sqlite3  ◀── VACUUM INTO .tmp + os.replace + vol.commit()
132	                    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
133	                                   Modal Volume "faultline-db" (v1)
134	```
135	
136	- **Single writer.** `Store` is a Modal class with `max_containers=1`. It is the only process that ever
137	  opens the database, so SQLite's locking assumptions hold trivially and nobody needs `reload()` — the
138	  reader *is* the writer. `min_containers=1` keeps it warm (an SSE poll must not pay a cold start plus a
139	  restore). `@modal.concurrent(max_inputs=32)` lets many `api` polls and one run's appends interleave; a
140	  `threading.Lock` serialises access to the single `sqlite3` connection (ops are sub-millisecond).
141	- **Hot copy on local disk.** The live file is `/tmp/faultline.sqlite3` on the container's own disk with
142	  ordinary SQLite settings (`journal_mode=WAL`, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `foreign_keys=ON`,
143	  `busy_timeout=5000`). No FUSE questions.
144	- **Checkpoint.** `VACUUM INTO '/data/faultline.sqlite3.tmp'` writes a complete, consistent, single-file
145	  copy; `os.replace()` renames it over `/data/faultline.sqlite3`; `vol.commit()` publishes it. Because
146	  only complete files are ever renamed into place, a background commit can never capture a half-written
147	  database. Triggers: after every `run.finished`, every 15 s while there are unsaved writes, and in
148	  `@modal.exit`. A `checkpoint_now` local entrypoint forces one for evidence/export.
149	- **Restore.** `@modal.enter` copies `/data/faultline.sqlite3` → `/tmp` if present (else creates an empty
150	  DB), then applies migrations: `PRAGMA user_version` gates `harness/migrations/000N_*.sql`, applied in
151	  order inside one transaction each.
152	- **Durability model.** Completed runs are durable as soon as their post-`run.finished` checkpoint commits
153	  (seconds). If the Store container dies mid-run, at most the last ≤15 s of that run's events are lost
154	  from the Volume — and they are not lost from the system: `append_events` is idempotent on
155	  `(run_id, seq)` (`INSERT OR IGNORE`), and `run_episode` re-sends its complete in-memory event list at
156	  `run.finished`, which back-fills any gap. A redeploy briefly overlaps old and new Store containers; the
157	  same idempotent re-send covers the overlap for runs that finish, and the ≤15 s window is documented for
158	  runs that don't.
159	- **Ids.** `<prefix>_<hex ms timestamp><12 hex random>` (sortable, no extra dependency): `c_`, `r_`, `m_`,
160	  `b_`, `l_`. User ids are minted by the browser as `u_<uuid4>`.
161	- **Migration from the current code.** `harness/store.py` today is `RunStore` over the Modal Dict
162	  `faultline-runs` (one whole `RunRecord` per run, re-put on every event, 200-entry index). The
163	  `RunStore` interface (`get / create / list_runs`) is kept as a thin client over `Store` so `loop.py`
164	  and `api.py` change minimally; the loop switches from whole-record puts to `append_events` per step
165	  plus a full idempotent re-send at `run.finished`; the Dict is dropped.
166	- **Ops.** `modal volume get faultline-db faultline.sqlite3 runs/` yields a consistent file for local
167	  `sqlite3` inspection and for the evidence folder. Retention: none for the take-home (a `DELETE`
168	  archives; nothing is hard-deleted).
169	- **Known limits (accepted).** One Store container; full-file snapshot per checkpoint. Comfortable to
170	  hundreds of MB; past that, ship incrementally (Litestream-style, or `modal-mosql`) or move to Postgres.
171	  A single-writer actor is also a throughput ceiling (thousands of events/s — orders of magnitude above
172	  need).
173	
174	### 4.4 Schema (`services/agent-harness/harness/migrations/0001_init.sql`)
175	
176	```sql
177	-- Faultline harness store. All timestamps ISO-8601 UTC ("…Z"). JSON columns hold pydantic dumps
178	-- from faultline_common.schemas. This file is canonical once it lands; until then, this doc is.
179	PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;
180	
181	CREATE TABLE users (
182	  id            TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                    -- u_<uuid4>, minted by the browser (§6)
183	  created_at    TEXT NOT NULL,
184	  last_seen_at  TEXT NOT NULL,
185	  user_agent    TEXT
186	);
187	
188	CREATE TABLE conversations (
189	  id            TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                    -- c_…
190	  user_id       TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id),
191	  scenario_id   TEXT NOT NULL,
192	  title         TEXT NOT NULL,                       -- defaults to the scenario title; PATCHable
193	  created_at    TEXT NOT NULL,
194	  updated_at    TEXT NOT NULL,                       -- bumped on every run event (drives list order)
195	  archived_at   TEXT                                 -- soft delete
196	);
197	CREATE INDEX ix_conversations_user ON conversations(user_id, updated_at DESC);
198	
199	CREATE TABLE runs (
200	  id              TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                  -- r_…
201	  conversation_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES conversations(id),
202	  user_id         TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id),-- denormalised for ownership checks
203	  scenario_id     TEXT NOT NULL,
204	  model           TEXT NOT NULL,
205	  seed            INTEGER,
206	  max_steps       INTEGER NOT NULL,
207	  task_prompt     TEXT NOT NULL,                     -- what the user turn actually contained
208	  episode_id      TEXT,                              -- sandbox-env episode (set after reset)
209	  status          TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('queued','running','ok','error','truncated')),
210	  score           REAL,                              -- 0..100 from evaluate
211	  evaluation_json TEXT,                              -- EvaluateResponse (checks, tests, ledger)
212	  input_tokens    INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
213	  output_tokens   INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
214	  error           TEXT,
215	  created_at      TEXT NOT NULL,
216	  started_at      TEXT,
217	  finished_at     TEXT
218	);
219	CREATE INDEX ix_runs_conversation ON runs(conversation_id, created_at);
220	CREATE INDEX ix_runs_live ON runs(status) WHERE status IN ('queued','running');
221	
222	-- Append-only event log: the source of truth for replay, SSE resume and evidence export.
223	CREATE TABLE events (
224	  run_id  TEXT    NOT NULL REFERENCES runs(id),
225	  seq     INTEGER NOT NULL,                          -- 0-based; == Event.id == SSE id / Last-Event-ID
226	  ts      TEXT    NOT NULL,
227	  type    TEXT    NOT NULL,                          -- EventType (run.started … log, llm.call, turn.thinking)
228	  step    INTEGER,
229	  data    TEXT    NOT NULL,                          -- JSON: Event.data
230	  PRIMARY KEY (run_id, seq)
231	) WITHOUT ROWID;
232	
233	-- Transcript projection in Anthropic message shape: what the UI renders and what the model saw.
234	CREATE TABLE messages (
235	  id              TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                  -- m_…
236	  conversation_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES conversations(id),
237	  run_id          TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES runs(id),
238	  seq             INTEGER NOT NULL,                  -- order within the conversation
239	  role            TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('user','assistant')),
240	  step            INTEGER,                           -- harness step that produced it (NULL for the task prompt)
241	  created_at      TEXT NOT NULL,
242	  UNIQUE (conversation_id, seq)
243	);
244	
245	CREATE TABLE blocks (
246	  id           TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                     -- b_…
247	  message_id   TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES messages(id),
248	  seq          INTEGER NOT NULL,                     -- order within the message
249	  type         TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (type IN ('text','thinking','tool_use','tool_result')),
250	  text         TEXT,                                 -- text / thinking / tool_result output (already capped upstream)
251	  tool_name    TEXT,                                 -- tool_use
252	  tool_use_id  TEXT,                                 -- tool_use and tool_result (join key)
253	  input_json   TEXT,                                 -- tool_use input
254	  is_error     INTEGER,                              -- tool_result
255	  exit_code    INTEGER,                              -- tool_result (run_command)
256	  duration_ms  INTEGER,                              -- tool_result
257	  fault_json   TEXT,                                 -- FaultFired echoed on the result, if any
258	  truncated    INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
259	  UNIQUE (message_id, seq)
260	);
261	CREATE INDEX ix_blocks_tool_use ON blocks(tool_use_id);
262	
263	-- One row per messages.create: the "LLM turn". Retries on 429/5xx get attempt > 1.
264	CREATE TABLE llm_calls (
265	  id                 TEXT PRIMARY KEY,               -- l_…
266	  run_id             TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES runs(id),
267	  step               INTEGER NOT NULL,
268	  attempt            INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
269	  model              TEXT NOT NULL,
270	  request_id         TEXT,                           -- anthropic `request-id` response header
271	  stop_reason        TEXT,                           -- end_turn | tool_use | max_tokens | …
272	  input_tokens       INTEGER,
273	  output_tokens      INTEGER,
274	  cache_read_tokens  INTEGER,
275	  cache_write_tokens INTEGER,
276	  started_at         TEXT NOT NULL,
277	  duration_ms        INTEGER,
278	  error              TEXT,
279	  UNIQUE (run_id, step, attempt)
280	);
281	
282	PRAGMA user_version = 1;
283	```
284	
285	Relationships: `users 1─∞ conversations 1─∞ runs 1─∞ events`; `runs 1─∞ messages 1─∞ blocks`;
286	`runs 1─∞ llm_calls`. `events` is the source of truth; `messages`/`blocks`/`llm_calls` are projections
287	written in the same transaction (dropping them is scope cut #5 in the plan — the web reducer can rebuild
288	a transcript from events alone).
289	
290	### 4.5 Event → transcript projection
291	
292	Performed inside `Store.append_events`, per event, in the transaction that inserts the event row:
293	
294	| Event | Effect |
295	|---|---|
296	| `run.started` | `runs.status='running'`, `started_at`; `conversations.updated_at` |
297	| `episode.reset` | `runs.episode_id`; user message (`step NULL`) with one `text` block = `task_prompt` |
298	| `turn.thinking`, `turn.text`, `tool.call` (same `step`) | one assistant message for that step (created on first block); blocks appended in event order: `thinking`, `text`, `tool_use{tool_name, tool_use_id, input_json}` |
299	| `tool.result` (same `step`) | one user message for that step (Anthropic requires tool results in a user turn); `tool_result{tool_use_id, text=output, is_error, exit_code, duration_ms, fault_json}` |
300	| `llm.call` | `llm_calls` row; running totals into `runs.input_tokens/output_tokens` |
301	| `episode.evaluated` | `runs.score`, `runs.evaluation_json`; assistant message with the submit summary |
302	| `run.finished` | `runs.status`, `finished_at`, `error`, final usage; `conversations.updated_at`; marks the DB dirty for checkpoint |
303	| `fault.fired`, `workspace.diff`, `log` | event row only; the UI reads them from the event log |
304	
305	Rebuilding the model's view of step *n*: take the conversation's messages for the run with `seq` up to
306	the assistant message of step *n − 1* plus its tool-result message, expand blocks in `seq` order, map
307	`text/thinking/tool_use/tool_result` one-to-one onto Anthropic content blocks. The UI instead groups each
308	`tool_result` under its `tool_use` by `tool_use_id` and renders it as a tool chip inside the assistant turn.
309	
310	## 5. HTTP contracts
311	
312	### 5.1 Harness API (`faultline-harness/api`, CORS `*`, all routes below `/scenarios` and `/health` require `X-Faultline-User`)
313	
314	| Route | Body → Response |
315	|---|---|
316	| `GET /health` | `Health{svc:"harness", ok, model_default, sandbox_env_url, has_provider_key:false, detail:{store:{ok,last_checkpoint_at}}}` |
317	| `GET /scenarios` | proxied from sandbox-env (public fields only) |
318	| `GET /me` | upsert → `{user_id, conversations: n}` |
319	| `GET /conversations` | `[{id, title, scenario_id, created_at, updated_at, last_run:{id,status,score}|null}]` newest first, archived excluded |
320	| `POST /conversations` | `{scenario_id, title?}` → `Conversation` |
321	| `GET /conversations/{id}` | `{conversation, runs:[RunSummary], messages:[Message{blocks:[Block]}]}` |
322	| `PATCH /conversations/{id}` | `{title?, archived?}` → `Conversation` |
323	| `DELETE /conversations/{id}` | soft archive → `{archived:true}` |
324	| `POST /conversations/{id}/runs` | `{model?, seed?, max_steps?, task_prompt?}` → `{run_id}` (202) |
325	| `POST /runs` | `{scenario_id, model?, seed?, max_steps?}` → creates a conversation then a run → `{run_id, conversation_id}` |
326	| `GET /runs/{id}` | `RunRecord` (record + all events + evaluation + usage) |
327	| `GET /runs/{id}/events` | SSE; `id:` = `seq`; honours `Last-Event-ID`; server closes at ~110 s |
328	
329	Ownership: every `/conversations/*` and `/runs/*` route resolves the resource, compares `user_id` with
330	the header, and returns 404 on mismatch (not 403 — no existence oracle).
331	
332	Wire shapes (JSON; the pydantic models in `faultline_common.schemas` and `apps/web/src/lib/types.ts`
333	mirror these field for field; DB columns `input_json` / `fault_json` / `evaluation_json` are parsed
334	into `input` / `fault` / `evaluation` on the wire):
335	
336	```ts
337	type User                = { id: string; created_at: string; last_seen_at: string }
338	type Conversation        = { id: string; user_id: string; scenario_id: string; title: string;
339	                             created_at: string; updated_at: string; archived_at: string | null }
340	type ConversationSummary = { id: string; title: string; scenario_id: string; created_at: string; updated_at: string;
341	                             last_run: { run_id: string; status: RunStatus; score: number | null } | null }
342	type Block               = { id: string; seq: number; type: 'text' | 'thinking' | 'tool_use' | 'tool_result';
343	                             text?: string | null; tool_name?: string | null; tool_use_id?: string | null;
344	                             input?: Record<string, unknown> | null; is_error?: boolean | null; exit_code?: number | null;
345	                             duration_ms?: number | null; fault?: FaultFired | null; truncated: boolean }
346	type Message             = { id: string; conversation_id: string; run_id: string; seq: number;
347	                             role: 'user' | 'assistant'; step?: number | null; created_at: string; blocks: Block[] }
348	type LlmCall             = { id: string; run_id: string; step: number; attempt: number; model: string;
349	                             request_id?: string | null; stop_reason?: string | null;
350	                             input_tokens?: number | null; output_tokens?: number | null;
351	                             cache_read_tokens?: number | null; cache_write_tokens?: number | null;
352	                             started_at: string; duration_ms?: number | null; error?: string | null }
353	type RunSummary          = { run_id: string; status: RunStatus; scenario_id: string; model: string;
354	                             created_at: string; finished_at: string | null; events: number; score: number | null }
355	// RunRecord gains three optional fields: conversation_id?, user_id?, task_prompt?
356	// Event.data for the two new types:
357	//   'llm.call'      { attempt, model, stop_reason, usage: { input_tokens, output_tokens,
358	//                     cache_read_input_tokens?, cache_creation_input_tokens? }, request_id?, duration_ms }
359	//   'turn.thinking' { text }
360	// Responses:
361	//   GET  /me                        → { user_id: string; conversations: number }
362	//   GET  /conversations             → ConversationSummary[]
363	//   POST /conversations             → Conversation
364	//   GET  /conversations/{id}        → { conversation: Conversation; runs: RunSummary[]; messages: Message[] }
365	//   PATCH /conversations/{id}       → Conversation          DELETE → { archived: true }
366	//   POST /conversations/{id}/runs   → { run_id: string; conversation_id: string }
367	//   POST /runs                      → { run_id: string; conversation_id?: string }   (conversation_id once Store lands)
368	```
369	
370	Until the Store lands, the web app sends `X-Faultline-User` anyway and treats 404 on `/me` and
371	`/conversations` as "history unavailable" (rail hidden, runs still work through `POST /runs`).
372	
373	### 5.2 Sandbox-env (gym + MCP)
374	
375	Unchanged from `PLAN.md` §2.2: REST `POST /episodes` (reset), `GET /episodes/{id}` (observe),
376	`POST /episodes/{id}/evaluate`, `DELETE /episodes/{id}`, `GET /scenarios`, `GET /health`; MCP tools
377	`run_command`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `list_dir` at `/mcp`, episode selected by the
378	`X-Faultline-Episode` header so the model can never name another episode.
379	
380	### 5.3 Event stream
381	
382	Event types and payloads are defined in `faultline_common.schemas.Event` (`run.started`, `episode.reset`,
383	`turn.thinking`, `turn.text`, `tool.call`, `tool.result`, `llm.call`, `fault.fired`, `workspace.diff`,
384	`episode.evaluated`, `run.finished`, `log`). `Event.id` is the 0-based sequence within the run and doubles
385	as the SSE id, the `events.seq` primary key, and the resume cursor. Events are produced by exactly one
386	writer per run (`run_episode`) and are never edited.
387	
388	### 5.4 Reliability event contract (confirmed with the harness lead 2026-09-12 ~22:45 EDT)
389	
390	Canonical: `packages/common/faultline_common/schemas.py` (the `Event` docstring lists every data
391	shape), labels in `docs/error-taxonomy.md`, injector behaviour in `services/sandbox-env/FAULTS.md`.
392	The UI is a ten-second reliability read — *task → fault (simulated or real, which layer) → did the
393	command execute (executed / failed / not executed / unknown are four different answers) → recovery
394	(agent actions vs harness retries; did the sandbox survive) → diff + independent checks* — and it
395	**never derives an outcome from error text**: anything absent renders as "unknown" with the raw
396	payload expandable. Fields (all additive):
397	
398	| Where | Field | Meaning |
399	|---|---|---|
400	| `tool.result.data` | `outcome: executed \| failed \| not_executed \| unknown` | executed = ran and succeeded; failed = ran and reported failure; not_executed = refused/short-circuited before the sandbox saw it; unknown = may or may not have run (ack lost, transport, worker interrupted) |
401	| `tool.result.data` | `error_class` (iff `is_error`): `{origin: injected\|staged\|real, layer: boundary\|filesystem\|sandbox\|transport\|harness\|model\|gym, code, kind?, label, outcome_known, side_effect_applied?, detail?}` | provenance and layer; `label` is a fixed string rendered verbatim; `error_code` kept for compatibility; raw text stays in `output` |
402	| `tool.result.data` | `attempts`, `sandbox: {id, alive}` | harness-level retries of this call (1 = none); identity/liveness of the workspace that answered |
403	| `fault.fired.data` | `origin` (injected \| staged, never real), `layer` (boundary for injected, filesystem for staged), `description` | one plain sentence per fault |
404	| `episode.reset.data` | `sandbox_id` (short), `attempt` | workspace identity; >1 when re-provisioned |
405	| `episode.sandbox` (new) | `{sandbox_id, status: alive\|terminated\|replaced, reason, step}` | the environment noticed the worker changed |
406	| `interruption` (new) | `Interruption {step?, layer, code, label, tool_use_id?, tool?, path?, outcome_known, planned, resumed, worker_generation, at, detail?}` | always real; `planned: true` when a scenario `harness_faults` entry triggered it |
407	| `run.resumed` (new) | `{worker_generation, resumed_from_event_id, dangling_tool_use_id?, resumed_at}` | a fresh worker picked the run up |
408	| `run.finished.data` | `evaluation_status: ok\|failed\|skipped`, `evaluation_error?`, `steps`, `score?` | a run that could not be graded is `unevaluated`, never "ok" |
409	| `RunStatus` | `+ unevaluated`, `+ interrupted` | "ok" now implies a score |
410	| `RunRecord` | `error_class?`, `interruptions[]`, `worker_generation` | why the run ended as it did |
411	| `GET /scenarios` | `checks: [{id, description, weight}]`, `faults_public: [{kind, origin, layer, description}]`, `harness_faults: [{kind: worker_crash\|transport_abort, tool, path, nth, after_ms}]` | intended outcome and fault classes known before the run; the agent never sees them |
412	| codes (final) | `ENOENT EACCES ETIMEDOUT EINVAL ESANDBOX ETRANSPORT EHARNESS EMODEL EGYM EINTERNAL ENOEPISODE` | `ETIMEDOUT` is ack-lost *or* a real client timeout — origin/layer tell them apart |
413	
414	Older records (e.g. `r_e9bc5c8c6739`) predate these fields; the UI resolves what it can from the
415	evaluate ledger (`outcome: ack_lost` ⇒ the write landed, `short_circuit` ⇒ nothing ran) and says
416	"unknown" otherwise. Web-side model: `apps/web/src/lib/reliability.ts` (`buildVerdict`), rendered by
417	`src/components/verdict/VerdictStrip.tsx`.
418	
419	## 6. Identity
420	
421	- **Minting.** `src/lib/identity.ts`: read `localStorage["faultline.user_id"]`, else the `faultline_uid`
422	  cookie, else mint `"u_" + crypto.randomUUID()`; write back to both. Cookie attributes:
423	  `SameSite=Lax; Secure; Max-Age=31536000; Path=/`. Not `HttpOnly` (JS writes it). All storage access is
424	  wrapped in try/catch; a browser that blocks storage still works for the session with an in-memory id.
425	- **Transport.** The harness API lives on a different origin from the SPA, so cookies are never sent to
426	  it. The id travels as `X-Faultline-User` on every request; the harness CORS config allows that header.
427	- **Why both stores.** localStorage is the primary (no size/attribute quirks, not sent anywhere);
428	  the cookie only exists so the id survives a "clear site data → local storage" that leaves cookies.
429	- **Threat model.** This is scoping, not authentication (explicit non-goal). Ids are 122-bit random and
430	  never listed, so they are unguessable in practice, but anyone holding one can read that browser's
431	  transcripts. Nothing sensitive is stored: scenarios are bundled, the workspace is a fixture, and the
432	  provider key never leaves `run_episode`. `POST …/runs` is rate-limited per id.
433	
434	## 7. Web application
435	
436	### 7.1 Stack
437	
438	Vite 8 + React 19 + TypeScript 6, Tailwind v4 (`@tailwindcss/vite`), shadcn v4 CLI (style `base-nova`
439	on Base UI, `neutral` base colour, CSS variables, the `cn` helper package, lucide icons) for primitives,
440	Beautiful UI for the AI-native composites, vitest (jsdom). No client router: the URL carries
441	`?c=<conversation_id>`, `?run=<run_id>` or `?demo=<scenario_id>`. **Node ≥ 22.12 is required** (Vite 8
442	`^20.19 || >=22.12`, vitest 5 `^22.12 || ^24`; `.nvmrc` pins 22 and the Modal image installs Node 22).
443	Built into `dist/` inside the Modal image and served by a Modal Server (`python -m http.server`, or
444	`@modal.web_server` as the fallback), with `VITE_HARNESS_URL` baked in and `/config.json` as a runtime
445	override (precedence: injected `window.__FAULTLINE_CONFIG__` → `/config.json` → build env → default) so
446	the harness URL can change without a rebuild.
447	
448	### 7.2 Beautiful UI: what it is and how it is integrated
449	
450	`https://www.beautifului.dev` is an MIT-licensed gallery (© 2026 Shane Levine) of 21 React + Tailwind
451	components "for AI-native interfaces". It is **not** an npm package and **not** a shadcn registry: each
452	component has a *Copy code* button, and the TSX source is embedded in the page. Integration is therefore
453	copy-paste into `src/components/bui/` with a LICENSE file, plus three adaptations that the source makes
454	necessary:
455	
456	| Adaptation | Reason |
457	|---|---|
458	| icons → `lucide-react` | `SidebarNav` imports twelve icons from `@central-icons-react/*`, a paid set |
459	| unpublished atoms → shadcn | components import `@/components/atoms/{Button,StreamText,Shimmer,ValuePill,EntityChip}` and `@/components/primitives/GlideMenu`, which the gallery does not publish; shadcn `Button`, `Skeleton`, `Badge`, `DropdownMenu` stand in |
460	| drop `glimm` | `PromptBar` imports a shader/sound helper; the branch is deleted |
461	| `accent*` classes → `brand*` | shadcn already owns `accent` / `accent-foreground` (its subtle hover surface); Beautiful UI uses `accent` as the brand colour. Renaming on copy keeps both palettes intact |
462	
463	Components used and their role: `SidebarNav` (conversation rail), `PromptBar` (composer with scenario
464	`/` command and model picker), `StreamingText` (assistant prose), `ThinkingState` (per-step trace),
465	`ToolChips` (tool calls with output/exit code/duration/fault badge), `CodeBlock` (outputs and unified
466	diffs), `TaskRows` (score card), `LoadingState`, `DiffTable` (workspace file status). No animation library
467	is required; the components use CSS transitions and `useLayoutEffect`.
468	
469	Theme bridge: Beautiful UI is written against its own Tailwind tokens (`ink`, `ink-2`, `ink-3`, `canvas`,
470	`surface`, `line`, `line-strong`, and `accent`, `accent-tint`, `accent-ink` → renamed `brand*`). They are
471	defined once in the existing `@theme inline` block of `src/index.css` as aliases of shadcn's CSS
472	variables (`--foreground`, `--muted-foreground`, `--background`, `--card`, `--border`, `--input`,
473	`--primary`, `--primary-foreground`), so both libraries follow the same light/dark palette (exact block
474	in `PLAN.md` §2.10.2).
475	
476	### 7.3 Layout, routes, state
477	
478	```
479	┌──────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
480	│ SidebarNav   │ header: title · scenario · model · status │ Workspace (Tabs)     │
481	│  + New run   │           step n/max · tokens · elapsed   │  Files (DiffTable)   │
482	│  search      │ ─────────────────────────────────────────│  Diffs (CodeBlock)   │
483	│  today       │ [user] task prompt                        │  Ledger              │
484	│   · lost-ack │ [assistant] StreamingText                 │                      │
485	│   · locked…  │    ThinkingState ▸ ToolChips (CodeBlock)  │                      │
486	│  yesterday   │      fault ⚡ · recovered ✓               │                      │
487	│   · …        │ [assistant] summary + TaskRows score card │                      │
488	│              │ ─────────────────────────────────────────│                      │
489	│ u_1a2b… ·    │ PromptBar  /scenario  model ▾  seed  Run  │                      │
490	│ Replay demo  │                                           │                      │
491	└──────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘
492	```
493	
494	- Landing (`/`): five bullets explaining the app, a centered composer (`/` picks a scenario, model and
495	  seed chips), and below it a wide shadcn data table of scenarios (TanStack Table v9, fixed layout)
496	  with separate columns for title, description, max turns, failure badges, Live run and Replay;
497	  every injected-fault scenario ships a real recorded replay; harness reachability is a composer hint
498	  and a line in the identity menu, not a card.
499	- URL state (no router, as scaffolded): no params = the landing page;
500	  `?c=<conversation_id>` = a persisted transcript; `?c=…&run=<run_id>` = that run inside its
501	  conversation (live tail if unfinished); `?demo=<scenario_id>` = bundled replay.
502	- One pure reducer (`src/lib/reducer.ts`, already present) builds the view model for live SSE, for
503	  `GET /runs/{id}` restore, and for replay of `public/demo/*.json`. Persisted transcripts from
504	  `GET /conversations/{id}` are mapped to the same view model, so every path renders through the same
505	  components.
506	- `src/lib/api.ts` (already present) reconnects SSE with `Last-Event-ID` and, after two failed opens,
507	  falls back to polling `GET /runs/{id}`; identity is added to it as a default header.
508	- Theme: light / dark / system, chosen from a radio group of icon buttons in the top bar and stored
509	  per browser (`faultline.theme`); "system" follows `prefers-color-scheme` live. The choice is applied
510	  by toggling the `dark` class on `<html>` (shadcn's dark variant) both from an inline pre-paint script
511	  in `index.html` and from `src/lib/theme.ts`, so the first paint is already right. Semantic colour
512	  tones are written as light/dark pairs so status badges keep contrast on both backgrounds.
513	- Workspace panel: shadcn Resizable panels (`react-resizable-panels` 4.x) on ≥1280 px — draggable
514	  handle, collapsible to zero, split persisted in localStorage, the top-bar toggle bound to the
515	  panel's imperative handle; an overlay sheet below that width. The Logs tab virtualizes its rows
516	  (`@tanstack/react-virtual`, rows measured after render because lines wrap) and follows the tail
517	  while a run is live.
518	- Verdict strip (`src/components/verdict/VerdictStrip.tsx`, model in `src/lib/reliability.ts`): the
519	  five-panel ten-second read described in §5.4, rendered above every transcript (live, persisted,
520	  replay); details and raw JSON expand per panel; the sandbox chip in the top bar shows workspace
521	  identity, worker generation and survival.
522	- Unified logging mirrors the server's JSON shape to the console with `svc:"web"`.
523	
524	## 8. Deployment and configuration
525	
526	| Item | Value |
527	|---|---|
528	| Workspace / environment | `appliedlabsai` / `local` → web functions at `https://appliedlabsai-local--<app>-<fn>.modal.run`; the static site is a Modal **Server**, whose URL is `https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-web-site.us-east.modal.direct` (read it back with `modal.Server.from_name("faultline-web", "site").get_url()`; the `.modal.run` shape answers 404 for Servers) |
529	| Deploy order | sandbox-env → harness (creates Volume `faultline-db` on first deploy) → web. Live URLs (2026-09-12): harness `https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-harness-api.modal.run`, sandbox-env `https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-sandbox-env-api.modal.run`, web `https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-web-site.us-east.modal.direct` |
530	| Secret | `anthropic-secret` (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, optional `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE`; name overridable via `FAULTLINE_ANTHROPIC_SECRET`), attached only to `run_episode` |
531	| Volume | `faultline-db` (v1), mounted at `/data` on `Store` only |
532	| Env | `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` (default `claude-haiku-4-5`), `SANDBOX_ENV_URL`, `HARNESS_URL`, `LOG_LEVEL` (see `.env.example`) |
533	| Warm containers | web Server, harness `api`, harness `Store` (`min_containers=1`) |
534	| Web image | Node 22 + pnpm; `pnpm i --frozen-lockfile && pnpm build`; `config.json` written from `HARNESS_URL` at container start |
535	| Sandbox guardrails | `timeout=1800`, `idle_timeout=600`, `block_network=True`, delete in `finally`, `reap` helper |
536	| Export | `modal run … ::checkpoint_now` then `modal volume get faultline-db faultline.sqlite3 runs/` |
537	
538	## 9. Decision log
539	
540	| # | Decision | Alternatives considered | Why |
541	|---|---|---|---|
542	| 1 | Three Modal apps with a strict secret boundary | one app | harness swappable, environment gradeable without trusting the agent, static UI; the provider key exists in exactly one function |
543	| 2 | Faults injected at the tool boundary, not inside the shell | `chmod`, deleting files live, network faults | deterministic, seedable, invisible to the agent, gradeable from a ledger; ack-lost is only expressible at the boundary |
544	| 3 | `spawn` + event log + reconnecting SSE | run the loop inside the request | Modal web requests cap at 150 s |
545	| 4 | Persistence = SQLite on a Modal Volume | Modal Dict only; hosted Postgres; Modal Dict as hot path + SQLite archive | user asked for it; zero external services; a single file is trivial to export as evidence |
546	| 5 | Single-writer `Store` actor (`max_containers=1`) | every function opens the DB on the Volume; per-run DB files | Volumes have no distributed locking and last-write-wins; one process owning the file removes every consistency question |
547	| 6 | Hot DB on local disk, `VACUUM INTO` snapshots renamed atomically onto the Volume | run SQLite directly on the FUSE mount | WAL/locking on FUSE is undocumented, and background commits are not multi-file snapshots; Modal's own example copies-then-commits |
548	| 7 | Volume v1 | v2 | v2 needs an explicit flag and is marked experimental in the 1.5.5 CLI; no benefit for one file |
549	| 8 | `events` as source of truth + `messages`/`blocks`/`llm_calls` projections | events only; projections only | replay/SSE need the log; the UI and "what did the model see" need the message shape; projections are a cheap same-transaction write and a listed scope cut |
550	| 9 | Identity via `X-Faultline-User` header, minted in the browser, mirrored to a cookie | cookie-only; server-minted id | the API is cross-origin so cookies never reach it; localStorage is the reliable store; the cookie is a backup |
551	| 10 | Beautiful UI by copy-paste with lucide/shadcn substitutions | Vercel AI Elements (a real registry); shadcn only | matches the requested look; MIT; the private deps are small and swappable; scope cut #4 if it drags |
552	| 11 | Haiku 4.5 default, Sonnet/Opus 5 allowlisted | Sonnet default | cost and latency for a demo; the ack-lost scenario is more interesting when a cheaper model sometimes fails it |
553	| 12 | No client router; conversation/run/demo in query params | `react-router-dom` | the scaffold already does this and it is enough for three screens; one less dependency in a static SPA |
554	| 13 | Node 22 pinned (`.nvmrc`, `engines`, Modal image) | stay on the machine default 21.6 | Vite 8 and vitest 5 refuse to start on 21.6 (`styleText` missing from `node:util`) |
555	
556	## 10. Open questions
557	
558	- Should the composer allow editing the task prompt, or only choosing a scenario? Current answer: editable,
559	  stored in `runs.task_prompt`, because it makes the "conversation" framing honest. Revisit if it invites
560	  prompt-injection-shaped demos that distract from the reliability story.
561	- Do we need per-user rate limiting beyond `POST …/runs`? Probably not for review traffic.
562	- Checkpoint cadence (15 s) vs. cost of `VACUUM INTO` as the DB grows — measure once real runs exist and
563	  record the number in `PLAN.md` §0.

11	## boundaries and correctness
12	
13	- keep three independent modal apps: `apps/web` for the browser, `services/agent-harness` for the model loop and persistence, `services/sandbox-env` for the gym, mcp tools and sandbox lifecycle. wire contracts live in `packages/common/faultline_common/schemas.py`.
14	- execute real shell commands in isolated modal sandboxes. keep `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the harness model worker via a modal secret; never expose it to the browser, command sandbox or logs. keep fault plans and grading authority out of model-controlled state.
15	- preserve the single-writer sqlite store; don't create another writer against its modal volume. see `ARCHITECTURE.md` for persistence and deployment details.
16	- use `docs/error-taxonomy.md` and `services/sandbox-env/FAULTS.md` for fault semantics. simulated errors, staged filesystem conditions and real interruptions must remain distinguishable. unknown execution is not failed execution; reconcile before retrying writes.
17	- prove harness recovery with a fresh worker continuing the same run and surviving workspace. browser reconnection or an emitted resume event alone is not proof. persist recovery state before side effects; verify fault timing rather than assuming a sleep establishes it.
18	- make the ui explain task, failure, execution status, recovery and verification. derive labels from structured events; label recorded replays and keep pending or unavailable grading distinct from success.
19	
20	## verification
21	
22	use python 3.11+ and node 22 (`apps/web/.nvmrc`), with pnpm. from the repo root, using the existing service virtualenvs:
23	
24	```sh
25	services/agent-harness/.venv/bin/python -m pytest services/agent-harness/tests
26	services/sandbox-env/.venv/bin/python -m pytest services/sandbox-env/tests
27	pnpm --dir apps/web test
28	pnpm --dir apps/web build
29	pnpm --dir apps/web lint
30	```
31	
32	- run checks relevant to the change. unit tests don't establish deployed behavior.
33	- for backend verification, read `.claude/skills/verify-faultline/SKILL.md`; for ui changes, exercise the actual browser journey. save commands, outputs, file evidence and screenshots under `runs/<timestamp>/`.
34	- check outcomes against actual files and execution evidence, not the model's success claim or a score alone. include a failing control when changing grading. clean up only your test resources and preserve evidence after cleanup.
35	
36	## submission
37	
38	follow `Platform_SWE_take-home_assignment.pdf`: target 1–2 hours, hard limit 8; track actual time and stop implementation at the limit. deliver a working hosted prototype with bundled examples, github repo, short rationale, approximately five-minute self-recorded video and genuine ai transcripts. record tradeoffs in `RATIONALE.md`; never invent results, transcripts or time spent. the recruiter's email specifies greenhouse submission.

582	## 7. Scope cuts (drop in this order if time runs out)
583	
584	1. `gauntlet` scenario → keep 3 single-fault scenarios.
585	2. Per-run model override → env default only.
586	3. Live unified diffs → file status list only (added/modified/deleted).
587	4. Beautiful UI composites → plain shadcn (Card/Collapsible/Table) driven by the same reducer; resizable workspace → fixed column; virtualized logs → capped list.
588	5. `messages`/`blocks`/`llm_calls` projection → store `events` only; the web reducer builds the transcript.
589	6. SSE → plain 1 s polling of `GET /runs/{id}`.
590	7. `@app.server` static hosting → `@modal.web_server` (or, last resort, serve the built SPA from the harness FastAPI).
591	8. Cross-review verify pass → single reviewer.
592	9. Video polish → screen recording with narration, one take.
593	10. **Never cut**: secret boundary, the ack-lost fault with verification grading, bundled replay/demo mode, `runs/` evidence, persisted conversations (SQLite on the Volume + browser identity — it is the product's memory).

596	
597	## 8. Risks & mitigations
598	
599	| Risk | Mitigation |
600	|---|---|
601	| Modal 150 s web request cap kills long SSE | spawn + Store + reconnecting SSE (§2.3); polling fallback |
602	| MCP SDK API drift (fastmcp 4.x / mcp 2.x) | pin exact versions verified by research; adapter kept tiny (`mcp_client.py`, `mcp_tools.py`) |
603	| `@app.server` semantics surprise (URL shape, cold 503s) | `min_containers=1`; fallback to `@modal.web_server` |
604	| Sandbox create latency (3–10 s) makes runs feel slow | show `episode.reset` progress event; pre-warm optional |
605	| Haiku ignores "verify before retry" → low scores look like bugs | UI explains checks; allow Sonnet override; prompt tuned once with evidence in `runs/` |
606	| Provider key/quota missing at review time | bundled replay + clear health banner |
607	| Stray sandboxes cost money | idle/timeouts + `finally` delete + `reap` helper |
608	| Time overrun (8 h cap) | §7 cuts; §9 time log reviewed every 90 min |
609	| SQLite on a FUSE-mounted Volume (locking, mmap, background commit capturing a half-written file) | hot DB on container-local disk; `VACUUM INTO` snapshot renamed atomically on the Volume then `commit()`; single writer (`max_containers=1`); same copy-then-commit pattern as Modal's own Datasette example (§2.9) |
610	| Store container replaced mid-run (redeploy / crash) → tail of an in-flight run lost | idempotent `(run_id, seq)` inserts; `run_episode` re-sends its full event list at `run.finished`; checkpoint in `@modal.exit`; ≤15 s window documented |
611	| Beautiful UI deps we don't have (Central Icons, `glimm`, `liveline`, unpublished atoms) and its `accent` token clashing with shadcn's | copy only the §2.10.1 components; lucide for icons, shadcn for atoms; delete the shader branch in PromptBar; rename `accent*`→`brand*` (§2.10.2); §7 cut #4 if it drags |
612	| Node version drift (Vite 8 / vitest 5 need ≥ 22.12; machine default is 21.6) | `.nvmrc`=22, `engines` in `package.json`, Node 22 in the Modal web image |
613	| Anonymous browser id is spoofable | non-goal (§1); nothing sensitive stored; ids unguessable in practice; rate-limit `POST …/runs` per id |

# Faultline

**A mini agent harness with a browser view, where the environment fights back.**

A Claude agent runs *real* shell commands — reading files, editing code, running tests — inside an
isolated sandbox. The environment deliberately injects the failures real tool-using agents meet in
production: a file that isn't there, a write that is denied, and a write that **succeeds but whose
response times out**. The browser shows every command, its output, the resulting file changes, and
whether the agent recovered. The environment is framed as a tiny RL gym
(`reset / step / observe / evaluate`) so recovery behaviour is *graded*, not just eyeballed.

> Take-home for Anthropic Platform SWE · Theme 3 (Systems & Reliability) with a Theme 4 (Evaluation) twist.
> Live demo: **WEB_URL_TBD** · Rationale: [RATIONALE.md](RATIONALE.md) · Plan/log: [PLAN.md](PLAN.md)

## Try it in 60 seconds

1. Open the live URL. Pick a scenario (each card lists which fault kinds are in play, not where or when).
2. Press **Run**. Watch the timeline: assistant reasoning, tool calls, outputs, fault badges, diffs.
3. Read the score card: hidden tests (60%) + recovery checks (40%), e.g. *"verified the file before
   re-writing after the lost ack"* and *"exactly one changelog entry"*.
4. No API quota? Press **Replay demo** — a recorded run plays back with no backend.

## Architecture

```
 browser  ──HTTPS JSON + SSE──▶  agent-harness (Modal)  ──MCP over HTTP + gym REST──▶  sandbox-env (Modal)
 apps/web static on Modal        api: no secrets                                       controller · fault plan · grader
                                 run_episode: the only fn with ANTHROPIC_API_KEY            │ Sandbox.exec
                                                                                            ▼
                                                                                 Modal Sandbox (/workspace, no network)
```

Three separately deployed Modal apps, three trust boundaries:

| Component | Holds | Never sees |
|---|---|---|
| `apps/web` | UI, replay data | secrets, fault plan |
| `services/agent-harness` | the model loop (`run_episode` has the provider key), run records, SSE | workspace files (only via MCP), fault plan |
| `services/sandbox-env` | gym state, fault plan, ledger, grader, Sandbox lifecycle | the provider key |
| Modal Sandbox | the agent's shell and files | everything above; outbound network is blocked |

Faults are applied **at the tool boundary** inside `sandbox-env`, so the shell the agent runs in
cannot detect or read the plan. `ack_lost` performs the write, then withholds the acknowledgement —
the agent must read the file back before deciding whether to retry. Because the task is an
append-style edit, a careless retry produces a duplicate that the grader catches.

## Simulated vs. real failures

The agent is never told whether a failure was injected. Everything else is: every failing tool
result carries an `error_class` (`origin: injected | staged | real`, the `layer` that really failed,
a fixed human label) and an `outcome` (`executed | failed | not_executed | unknown`), fault events
say whether the environment short-circuited the call or withheld an acknowledgement, and runs that
could not be graded end as `unevaluated` or `interrupted`, never as `ok` without a score.
`services/sandbox-env/FAULTS.md` documents what each injector really does on disk;
`docs/error-taxonomy.md` is the contract the UI renders. The `worker-crash` scenario is the real
counterpart of `lost-ack`: the harness process is actually killed while a write is in flight and a
fresh worker resumes the run from the persisted events.

## Repo layout

```
apps/web/                 Vite + React + shadcn/ui; modal_app.py serves the built site via a Modal Server
services/agent-harness/   FastAPI api (POST /runs, GET /runs/{id}, SSE events) + run_episode model loop
services/sandbox-env/     FastAPI gym (reset/observe/evaluate) + fastmcp tools (run_command/read_file/write_file/list_dir)
packages/common/          faultline_common: pydantic wire contracts + unified JSON logging
scripts/                  deploy.sh, smoke_roundtrip.py, run_episode_cli.py, fault_proofs.py, export_demo.py
runs/                     (gitignored) evidence from every run against Modal
PLAN.md                   the living plan: checklists, done conditions, scope cuts, time log
```

## Running it yourself

Prereqs: Python 3.11, `uv`, Node ≥ 20 + pnpm, a Modal account (`modal token new`), and a Modal secret
`anthropic-secret` with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE` (org-scoped keys must send the workspace id header; the harness does).

```bash
modal secret create anthropic-secret ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-... ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE=wrkspc_... -e local
scripts/deploy.sh local          # sandbox-env → harness → web, prints the three URLs
```

Individual pieces:

```bash
python scripts/smoke_roundtrip.py --base https://<sandbox-env-url>     # one real shell/MCP round trip, evidence in runs/
python scripts/run_episode_cli.py --scenario lost-ack                  # a full model run, live transcript, evidence in runs/
cd services/sandbox-env && uv run pytest                               # fault engine + grader tests (no Modal needed)
cd apps/web && pnpm i && pnpm dev                                      # UI against VITE_HARNESS_URL
```

Configuration: `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` (default `claude-haiku-4-5`; the UI can pick Sonnet 5 / Opus 5),
`SANDBOX_ENV_URL`, `HARNESS_URL`, `LOG_LEVEL=debug` for argument/output dumps. See `.env.example`.

## Verifying it

`.claude/skills/verify-faultline/` is the repo's verification skill: it drives the deployed gym over
MCP exactly as the harness does (careful and careless recovery, all three fault kinds, an optional
live model episode), reads the workspace files back, cross-checks the grader from those files and
the ledger, and keeps everything under `runs/<ts>_verify/` (commands, raw outputs, file evidence,
scores, pass/fail, and a hash manifest computed after cleanup).

```bash
services/sandbox-env/.venv/bin/python .claude/skills/verify-faultline/helpers/verify_backend.py --live
```

The browser flow is not covered yet; see `PLAN.md` §4 for what is verified and what is still open.

## Logging

Every service emits one JSON line per event with the same shape
(`ts svc lvl run_id episode_id step ev msg …`), so a single `run_id` can be followed from the browser
console through `modal app logs faultline-harness -e local` into `modal app logs faultline-sandbox-env -e local`.

## Status and limits

See [PLAN.md](PLAN.md) §0 for the status board and §7 for what was cut. Known limits: one sandbox per
episode (no pooling), Modal's 150 s web request cap is handled by spawning the run and streaming
events from a `modal.Dict`, and the fault matcher for raw shell commands is token-based (good enough
for `cat`, `sed -i`, `>>`; not a full shell parser).
# Faultline — design rationale

*Short written companion to the ~5 min video. Time spent: **TBD h** (see PLAN.md §9).*

## Why this theme, why this approach

I picked **Theme 3 (Systems & Reliability)** because the interesting failures in agent products are
not model failures but *tool-boundary* failures: the file isn't there, the write is refused, or —
the nasty one — the write happened but the acknowledgement never arrived. Most harness demos show
the happy path. I wanted a small, honest environment where those failures are first-class, visible,
and **graded**, so you can compare models and prompts on *how they recover*, not just on whether the
tests pass. That is the Theme 4 twist: the environment is a tiny gym (`reset / step / observe /
evaluate`) whose reward includes recovery quality.

## What is non-obvious

1. **Faults live outside the shell.** The fault plan, hit counters, ledger and grader sit in the
   `sandbox-env` service; the agent's commands run in a separate Modal Sandbox with no network. The
   shell cannot detect the plan, and the fault is applied at the tool boundary, so an injected
   `EACCES` looks exactly like a real one.
2. **Lost-ack + append is a detectable idempotency trap.** `ack_lost` performs the write and *then*
   times out the response. The task is an append-style edit, so a blind retry duplicates the entry.
   The grader checks both *"did you read the file back before writing again?"* (from the ledger) and
   *"is there exactly one entry?"* (from the file) — the behaviour and the outcome.
3. **The harness is replaceable; the environment is the product.** The step surface is MCP, so any
   MCP-speaking agent can play, and the grader never trusts the agent's own account of what happened.
4. **Simulated and real failures are different things, and the UI must say which.** The agent sees
   the same `ENOENT`/`504` either way; the ledger, events and run status carry provenance
   (`injected` / `staged` / `real`, which layer failed, whether the side effect happened). A real
   specimen forced this: a sandbox killed by a concurrent cleanup looked like an injector bug and the
   run ended "ok" with no score. Now it ends `interrupted` with `real: sandbox terminated`, and the
   `worker-crash` scenario kills the harness worker for real and resumes it.
5. **Secret boundary as a deliverable.** Only the model-calling Modal function receives the provider
   key. The web API, the browser and the sandbox provably don't (`/health` reports it, and the sandbox
   run of `env | grep -i anthropic` is part of the evidence).

## Key decisions and tradeoffs

| Decision | Alternative | Why this way |
|---|---|---|
| Three separately deployed Modal apps | one app, one process | mirrors real trust boundaries; each piece can be swapped; costs one URL hop |
| Per-episode Modal Sandbox with `block_network=True` | subprocess in the service container | real isolation and a real shell; costs ~seconds of provisioning per run |
| Faults intercepted at the tool boundary | real `chmod`/deletes inside the sandbox | works for the ack-lost case (impossible to fake inside the shell), deterministic hit counts, plan stays hidden; downside: raw-shell matching is token-based, not a shell parser |
| Spawned run + events in `modal.Dict` + reconnecting SSE | run inside the HTTP request | Modal caps web requests at 150 s; this also makes page refresh and replay free |
| Manual Anthropic tool loop | SDK tool runner | needed per-step hooks (observe after mutations, fault deltas, event stream) and no beta dependency |
| Haiku 4.5 default, Sonnet/Opus selectable | Opus everywhere | Haiku is fast and cheap enough for a reviewer to run many episodes; stronger models are one click away and the recovery grading makes the difference visible |
| Hidden tests uploaded only at evaluate time | tests in the workspace | the agent can't game the grader; visible tests still exist for the task itself |
| Bundled replay | live only | the assignment requires self-contained evaluation; replay runs with no backend and no quota |

## What I'd do with more time

- Fault kinds: partial writes (short write), clock skew, flaky test runner, rate-limited tool calls.
- Pooled warm sandboxes and filesystem snapshots for sub-second resets; batch mode to run N seeds ×
  M models and chart recovery rates per fault kind (the gym is already seedable).
- Trajectory export in a standard format so runs can feed evals or preference data.
- A "fault console" to author plans live and a diff-aware recovery detector (semantic, not
  path-token based).

## Use of AI

Built with Claude Code (Fable 5.1 as lead, three Opus agents for parallel implementation and
cross-review). My role: framing the idea, the trust-boundary split, the gym contract, the fault
semantics and grading rules, scenario design, and verification against real deployments. The
transcripts are included with the submission.

=================== docs/sandbox-env-ops.md
# sandbox-env: lifecycle, cost guardrails and measured latency

Owner: `services/sandbox-env`. Written 2026-09-12 23:1x UTC, after the Extend pass.
Nothing in the gym contract (PLAN.md §2.2) changed — the routes below are **additive ops routes**.
`POST /episodes`, `GET /episodes/{id}`, `POST /episodes/{id}/evaluate`, `DELETE /episodes/{id}`,
`GET /scenarios`, `GET /health` and `/mcp` are all byte-for-byte what they were.

## 1. Who reclaims a sandbox, in order

| # | Mechanism | When | Blast radius |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `DELETE /episodes/{id}` | the harness' `finally` | one episode |
| 2 | terminate-on-error inside `reset` / `evaluate` | reset fails after the sandbox exists (including `KeyboardInterrupt` and a failed Dict write), or `evaluate` finds the sandbox unreachable | one episode |
| 3 | TTL sweep | on every `reset` (throttled to once per `EPISODE_SWEEP_INTERVAL_S` per container) and via `POST /episodes/sweep` / `modal run …::sweep` | every episode older than `EPISODE_TTL_S` |
| 4 | `modal run -e local services/sandbox-env/modal_app.py::reap` | manual | **every** sandbox in `faultline-sandboxes` unless `--keep-active` |
| 5 | the sandbox's own `timeout=1800` / `idle_timeout=600` | always | one sandbox |

Environment knobs (all read at import): `EPISODE_TTL_S` (1800), `EPISODE_PURGE_S` (86400, when a
terminated record is dropped from the Dict), `EPISODE_SWEEP_INTERVAL_S` (300),
`EPISODE_SWEEP_MAX` (200 episodes per scan).

=================== docs/VIDEO_SCRIPT.md
# Faultline — 5-minute video script

Target 4:30–5:00. Screen: the deployed web app; one terminal tab for `modal app logs`.

## 0:00 – 0:30 · The problem
- "Agent demos show the happy path. In production the tool boundary fails: the file isn't there, the write is refused, or the write lands but the response times out."
- "Faultline is a mini agent harness with a browser view where the environment injects those failures on purpose — and grades whether the agent recovers."

## 0:30 – 1:30 · Architecture (show README diagram)
- Three Modal apps, three trust boundaries: browser → harness (only `run_episode` has the Anthropic key) → sandbox-env (gym + MCP; fault plan, ledger, grader) → Modal Sandbox (the agent's shell, no network).
- "The gym is reset / step / observe / evaluate. Step is MCP, so any MCP-speaking agent can play; evaluate runs hidden tests the agent never saw plus recovery checks from the ledger."
- One sentence on the 150 s Modal cap → spawn + Dict + resumable SSE.

## 1:30 – 4:00 · Live run: `lost-ack`
- Pick the scenario, keep Haiku, press Run. Narrate the timeline as it streams:
  - reset → files appear; the agent reads README/CHANGELOG.
  - the write to CHANGELOG.md hangs ~3 s and returns `504 … may or may not have completed` (fault badge).
  - "Watch what it does next." Ideal: read_file CHANGELOG.md → sees the entry landed → does not re-append → bumps version → pytest → submit.
  - Score card: tests 60% + checks (`verified_before_rewrite`, `no_duplicate_entry`, `version_bumped`).
- Switch to Workspace tab: diff shows exactly one 0.2.0 section. Logs tab: same `run_id` in every line; flash the terminal `modal app logs faultline-sandbox-env -e local` showing the `ack_lost` decision line.
- If time: run `locked-file` or show a replay where the agent appended twice and lost the `no_duplicate_entry` check.

## 4:00 – 4:45 · Decisions and tradeoffs
- Faults at the tool boundary, outside the shell (why not chmod: ack-lost is impossible to fake from inside; the plan stays hidden).
- Grade the ops, not the prose: the ledger, not the transcript.
- Haiku by default so reviewers can run many episodes; Sonnet/Opus one click away.
- Scope cuts made (see PLAN.md §7) and time spent.

## 4:45 – 5:00 · With more time
- More fault kinds (partial writes, flaky test runner), warm sandbox pools, batch runs across seeds × models with recovery-rate charts, trajectory export for evals.
=================== docs/error-taxonomy.md
# Error and status taxonomy (contract for the UI, the ledger and the evidence)

Two questions must be answerable for every failure a run shows: **who caused it** (`origin`) and
**which layer really failed** (`layer`). The agent never gets these; the UI always does.

## `ErrorClass` (on every `tool.result` with `is_error`, on `fault.fired`, on `RunRecord.error_class`)

```json
{"origin": "injected|staged|real", "layer": "boundary|filesystem|sandbox|transport|harness|model|gym",
 "code": "ENOENT|EACCES|ETIMEDOUT|EINVAL|ESANDBOX|ETRANSPORT|EHARNESS|EMODEL|EGYM|EINTERNAL|ENOEPISODE",
 "kind": "missing_file|denied_write|ack_lost|null", "label": "<fixed string below>",
 "outcome_known": true|false, "side_effect_applied": true|false|null, "detail": "…"}
```

| origin / layer | code(s) | label (render verbatim) | outcome_known | side effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| injected / boundary | ENOENT | `simulated: missing file (file still on disk)` | true | no |
| injected / boundary | EACCES | `simulated: write denied (nothing written)` | true | no |
| injected / boundary | ETIMEDOUT | `simulated: lost ack (write landed; response withheld)` | **false** | **yes** |
| staged / filesystem | ENOENT | `staged: file absent since reset` | true | n/a |

# Verify Faultline (backend flow)

Faultline is three Modal apps in environment `local`. This skill drives the two that are runnable
and deployed today: `faultline-sandbox-env` (gym REST + MCP tools, one Modal Sandbox per episode)
and `faultline-harness` (model loop + run API). It does **not** drive `apps/web` — that surface is
owned by another workstream and is tracked as unfinished in `PLAN.md` §0/§4 (V5, V8). Do not
document the browser path as verified until that section is extended.

Proof standard: a score from `evaluate` is a *claim*. Every run here also reads the workspace
files back through MCP, saves them, and re-checks the grader's assertions from the file contents
and the ledger independently. A green score with contradicting file evidence is a FAIL.

## Launch

The services are deployed; nothing runs locally. To (re)deploy from a clean checkout:

```bash
scripts/deploy.sh local        # sandbox-env → harness → web, prints URLs; needs Modal secret anthropic-secret
```

Ready when both health checks answer (`ok: true`, `has_provider_key: false`):

```bash
curl -s https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-sandbox-env-api.modal.run/health
curl -s https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-harness-api.modal.run/health
```

Cold start after a deploy is ~10 s for sandbox-env; the harness `api` keeps one warm container.
Wait for `/health` from the *new* version before trusting a run (a draining container can still
serve the previous code for a few seconds after `modal deploy` returns). Teardown of the deployment
is not part of verification; each run tears down only the episodes it created.

Override targets with `SANDBOX_ENV_URL` / `HARNESS_URL` (env) or `--sandbox-url` / `--harness-url`.

## Doctor

Read-only, run first whenever anything looks off:

```bash
services/sandbox-env/.venv/bin/python .claude/skills/verify-faultline/helpers/verify_backend.py --skip careful,careless,faults
```

It records `doctor.*` checks: sandbox-env `ok` + no provider key + catalogue has
`lost-ack`/`locked-file`/`missing-config` with no `fault_plan`/`hidden_tests` leak; harness `ok`
+ no provider key + `detail.sandbox_env_reachable: true`. Any FAIL here means the instance is not

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Deploy all three Faultline apps to Modal, in dependency order, and print their URLs.
# Usage: scripts/deploy.sh [modal-environment]   (default: local)
set -euo pipefail
ENV="${1:-${MODAL_ENVIRONMENT:-local}}"
ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)"
log() { printf '{"ts":"%s","svc":"deploy","lvl":"info","ev":"%s","msg":"%s"}\n' "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" "$1" "$2"; }

log deploy.start "environment=$ENV"
modal secret list -e "$ENV" | grep -q "${FAULTLINE_ANTHROPIC_SECRET:-anthropic-secret}" \
  || { log deploy.error "secret ${FAULTLINE_ANTHROPIC_SECRET:-anthropic-secret} missing in env $ENV"; exit 1; }

log deploy.sandbox_env "modal deploy services/sandbox-env/modal_app.py"
modal deploy -e "$ENV" "$ROOT/services/sandbox-env/modal_app.py"
SANDBOX_ENV_URL="${SANDBOX_ENV_URL:-$(python3 -c "import modal; print(modal.Function.from_name('faultline-sandbox-env','api',environment_name='$ENV').get_web_url())")}"
log deploy.sandbox_env.url "$SANDBOX_ENV_URL"

log deploy.harness "modal deploy services/agent-harness/modal_app.py"
SANDBOX_ENV_URL="$SANDBOX_ENV_URL" modal deploy -e "$ENV" "$ROOT/services/agent-harness/modal_app.py"
HARNESS_URL="${HARNESS_URL:-$(python3 -c "import modal; print(modal.Function.from_name('faultline-harness','api',environment_name='$ENV').get_web_url())")}"
log deploy.harness.url "$HARNESS_URL"

log deploy.web "modal deploy apps/web/modal_app.py"
HARNESS_URL="$HARNESS_URL" modal deploy -e "$ENV" "$ROOT/apps/web/modal_app.py"
WEB_URL="$(python3 -c "import modal; print(modal.Server.from_name('faultline-web','site',environment_name='$ENV').get_url())" 2>/dev/null || echo '(see modal deploy output above)')"
log deploy.web.url "$WEB_URL"

printf '\nSANDBOX_ENV_URL=%s\nHARNESS_URL=%s\nWEB_URL=%s\n' "$SANDBOX_ENV_URL" "$HARNESS_URL" "$WEB_URL"

cd /Users/sohamwaychal/Documents/anthropic; sed -n 60,96p docs/store-notes.md; echo '---'; grep -n "CHECK (status" services/agent-harness/harness/migrations/*.sql; ls services/agent-harness/harness/migrations/
Tool reply2026-09-12T23:17:42.988Z
* one `user` message with `step: null` — the task prompt, one `text` block;
* one `assistant` message **per step**: blocks in event order `thinking` → `text` → `tool_use`
  (`tool_name`, `tool_use_id`, `input` parsed, not a JSON string);
* one `user` message per step holding that step's `tool_result` blocks (`tool_use_id`, `text`,
  `is_error`, `exit_code` when the tool was `run_command`, `duration_ms`, `fault`, `truncated`).

Group a result under its call by `tool_use_id`; every `tool_use` in a finished run has exactly one
`tool_result` (asserted live: 32/32 on the gauntlet transcript). `messages[].seq` is per-conversation
and monotonic, so a conversation with several runs renders as one thread.

`fault.fired`, `workspace.diff` and `log` are **not** projected — read them from the event log as you
do today.

## Token counters

`llm.call.data.usage` now carries `cache_creation_input_tokens` as well as
`cache_read_input_tokens` (the gap flagged in `docs/harness-contract.md` is closed), and
`RunRecord.usage` carries all four counters. If you show a cost/token chip, note that
`input_tokens` is only the **uncached** tail once caching kicks in — e.g. the 27-step gauntlet run
reports `input_tokens: 8808` with `cache_read_input_tokens: 199090`. Summing `input_tokens` alone
now understates the context by an order of magnitude; render reads separately rather than adding
them in.

Measured on `claude-haiku-4-5` (`runs/20260912T230110Z_store/run_*/events.jsonl`): cache writes start
at the step where the conversation prefix first exceeds 4096 tokens (the model's minimum cacheable
prefix), and reads start on the step after that — step 7 of 12 on `missing-config`, step 5 of 27 on
`gauntlet`. Before this change every call reported `cache_read_input_tokens: 0`, so a UI that only
lights up on a non-zero read will now light up mid-run, not at all.

## Two things worth knowing

1. `GET /conversations/{id}` returns the whole transcript in one response (55 messages / 329 blocks
   for the gauntlet run ≈ 200 KB). There is no pagination yet; if that becomes a problem, ask and I
   will add `?after_seq=`.
2. A conversation's `updated_at` is bumped on every batch of events, so the rail re-orders while a
   run is streaming. `last_run` in the summary is the newest run of that conversation, keyed by `id`
   **and** `run_id`.

3. Boundary / correctness violations

  1. Anyone can kill every live sandbox over HTTP. api.py:175 POST /episodes/sweep?ttl_s=1 needs no auth and CORS allows *; checked, it terminated 2 of 2 live episodes. GET /episodes also publishes every episode id, and the X-Faultline-Episode header is the only MCP auth (PLAN §2.7 turned down tokens on the assumption ids are secret). Enforce a minimum ttl_s, and redact or gate the ops routes. High, low risk.
  2. reap can kill live sandboxes (the "safe reap" box at PLAN:447 is unchecked):
  3. It kills everything by default (modal_app.py:93), and a bare modal run modal_app.py does the same (:181).
  4. With --keep-active, a failed lookup spares nothing (:122-125).
  5. active_sandbox_ids only scans the first 200 records, terminated ones included (episodes.py:542,550).
  6. A sandbox that has been created (episodes.py:184) but not yet saved (:214) isn't spared.
  7. SKILL.md:127 needs updating with any fix. High, medium risk.
  8. The agent's files can reach the hidden-test run.
  9. .faultline_eval is in SKIP_DIRS (workspace.py:37), so anything the agent writes there doesn't show in observe.
  10. grader.py:331 uploads into that directory without clearing it, so an agent-written conftest.py there would load; so would a root conftest.py or pytest.ini.
  11. The directory name is readable in /opt/faultline_helper.py.
  12. Skipped tests aren't counted (grader.py:290).
  13. Fix: clear the directory before upload, add --confcutdir, and assert the expected number of hidden tests. Medium (not seen exploited), low risk.
  14. Unknown execution is recorded as failed.
  15. A WorkspaceError after dispatch (workspace.py:295-303), or a command timeout (mcp_tools.py:241), becomes outcome:error with EINTERNAL.
  16. The grader counts that as a plain failure (grader.py:84), not as maybe-applied.
  17. Timeout detection is string matching (workspace.py:298). Medium-high.
  18. State is persisted after side effects, with an unlocked read-modify-write.
  19. record() runs after the write and after the 3 s ack_lost sleep (mcp_tools.py:206-207).
  20. A retry or resumed worker arriving in that window can fire ack_lost again and lose a ledger row.
  21. If the container dies, the write has landed but no ledger row exists.
  22. Fix: persist a pending row before executing. Medium.
  23. Sleep assumed to set fault timing. worker-crash.json after_ms: 400 assumes the write has landed, but write_file p50 is 1123 ms (ops doc:63). Unverified. Medium.
  24. A sandbox lost during grading becomes a low score, not an ungraded run. grader.py:343 and :119 swallow the WorkspaceError, so the check reads "does not exist". Medium.
  25. Observe on a terminated episode returns the baseline marked "unchanged" (episodes.py:262-271), which hides the agent's edits. Low-medium.

No secret or fault-plan leak into the sandbox found (workspace.py:338,568; episodes.py:131).

    # Ops route (not part of the gym contract): what is running, how old it is, and whether its
    # sandbox is still alive. Cheap answer by default is NOT an option — "alive?" is the whole
    # question — so it polls, bounded by `limit`; `?probe=false` gives the Dict view with no RPCs.
    @app.get("/episodes")
    def list_episodes(probe: bool = True, limit: int = 200) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return episodes.list_episodes(probe=probe, limit=max(1, min(limit, 500)))

    # Manual trigger for the same sweep that runs on every reset. Handy in a demo, and it makes the
    # guardrail testable from outside without waiting for someone to press Run.
    @app.post("/episodes/sweep")
    def sweep_episodes(ttl_s: int | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return episodes.sweep(ttl_s=ttl_s)

    @app.get("/episodes/{episode_id}", response_model=ObserveResponse)
    def get_episode(episode_id: str) -> ObserveResponse:
        ctx_episode_id.set(episode_id)
        try:
            return episodes.observe(episode_id)
        except episodes.EpisodeNotFound:
            raise HTTPException(404, f"unknown episode {episode_id!r}") from None
        except WorkspaceError as exc:
            raise HTTPException(503, f"sandbox unavailable: {exc}") from None

    @app.post("/episodes/{episode_id}/evaluate", response_model=EvaluateResponse)
    def post_evaluate(episode_id: str) -> EvaluateResponse:
        ctx_episode_id.set(episode_id)
        try:
            return grader.evaluate(episode_id)
        except episodes.EpisodeNotFound:
            raise HTTPException(404, f"unknown episode {episode_id!r}") from None
        except WorkspaceError as exc:
            raise HTTPException(503, f"sandbox unavailable: {exc}") from None

    @app.delete("/episodes/{episode_id}")
    def delete_episode(episode_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
